researcher-0002:P1.1 [proved; polished] Component Measures
DEPS=KTZ Lemma 2.1,KTZ Lemma 6.2
Let $p$ be prime and let $d$ be an integer satisfying $100<d<p$. Let $f:\mathbb{F}_p^2\to\mathbb{F}_p$, and assign to every affine line $L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_p^2$ a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a connected subgraph of the accepted-incidence graph with minimum degree $k$, where an incidence $(x,L)$ is accepted when $P_L(x)=f(x)$. Put

$$
t=|T|,\qquad \mu(X)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\qquad \tau=\frac{t}{p(p+1)},\qquad C_*=2^{40}.
$$

Assume

$$
k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2.
$$

Then

$$
\mu(X)\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$

In particular, $t\ge k(p+1)/4$.

researcher-0002:P2.1 [proved; polished] Reaching Family
DEPS=P1,Chernoff bound,uniqueness of the affine line through two points
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1, there exists a set $R\subseteq T$ such that

$$
\frac{4t}{k}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{k},
$$

and, for every $L\in T$,

$$
\left|\left\{x\in N_G(L):\exists\,M\in R\setminus\{L\}\text{ with }(x,M)\in E\right\}\right|
>\frac12\deg_G(L).
$$

Moreover, $|R|\ge p+1$ and $|R|=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$ with absolute implied constants.

researcher-0002:P3.1 [proved; polished] Trivariate Explainer
DEPS=P2,KTZ Lemmas 2.2, 2.3, and 2.5
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1 and P2, put $r=|R|$ and

$$
D=16\left(\left\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\right\rfloor+1\right).
$$

There exists a nonzero squarefree polynomial $A\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$ such that

$$
A_Z\not\equiv0
$$

and

$$
A(M(s),P_M(s))\equiv0\qquad\text{for every }M\in R,
$$

where $M(s)$ is an affine parametrization of $M$. Moreover,

$$
D^2>256dr,
\qquad
D^2\le1024dr\le\frac{16384dt}{k}\le\frac{32768d\tau p^2}{k},
\qquad
\frac{D}{k}<\frac1{5000},
$$

so $D<k\le p<r$ and $D=O\!\left(\sqrt{d\tau p^2/k}\right)$.

researcher-0002:P4.1 [proved; polished] Linewise Extension
DEPS=P2,P3
Let $G=(X,T,E)$, $R$, $A$, and $D$ satisfy the hypotheses and conclusions of P2 and P3. Then, for every $L\in T$ and every affine parametrization $L(s)$,

$$
A(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0.
$$

researcher-0002:P5.1 [proved; polished] Exceptional-Root Cleanup
DEPS=P4,KTZ Lemmas 2.4 and 2.6
Under the hypotheses and notation of P4, write

$$
A=\sum_j a_j(X,Y)Z^j,
\qquad
H(X,Y)=\gcd_j a_j(X,Y),
\qquad
B=A/H.
$$

Then $B$ is squarefree, has total degree less than $p$, and satisfies

$$
B(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in T$ such that $H(L(s))\not\equiv0$. There exists a formal directional derivative $\mathcal{D}$ such that

$$
\gcd(B,\mathcal{D}B)=1.
$$

Define

$$
T_{\mathrm{triv}}=\{L\in T:H(L(s))\equiv0\}
$$

and

$$
T_{\mathrm{deg}}=
\left\{L\in T\setminus T_{\mathrm{triv}}:(\mathcal{D}B)(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
|T_{\mathrm{triv}}|\le D,
\qquad
|T_{\mathrm{deg}}|\le D(D-1),
$$

and every $L\in T\setminus(T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}})$ satisfies

$$
\left|\{x\in N_G(L):(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\}\right|\le D.
$$

Consequently, for

$$
E_{\mathrm{exc}}=
\left\{(x,L)\in E:L\in T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}}
\text{ or }(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\right\},
$$

one has

$$
|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|\le pD^2+Dt.
$$

researcher-0002:P6.1 [proved; polished] Clean Core
DEPS=P5
Under the hypotheses and notation of P5,

$$
\frac{|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|}{|E|}
\le\frac{D}{k}+\frac{16384dp}{k^2}
<\frac1{4000}.
$$

Set

$$
k_1=\left\lfloor\frac{k}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Starting from $(X,T,E\setminus E_{\mathrm{exc}})$ and iteratively deleting every vertex whose current degree is less than $k_1$ removes fewer than

$$
k_1(|X|+t)
\le2\frac{k_1}{k}|E|
\le\frac{|E|}{50}
$$

additional edges and leaves a nonempty subgraph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $k_1$.

researcher-0002:P7.1 [proved; polished] Simple Roots
DEPS=P6
Let $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ be the surviving graph from P6, and let $B$ and $\mathcal{D}$ be as in P5. Then every $x\in X_1$ satisfies

$$
B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$

researcher-0002:P8.1 [proved; polished] Polynomial Decoder
DEPS=P7,KTZ Lemmas 2.7, 6.1, 6.2, and 6.3
Under the hypotheses and notation of P1–P7, there exists a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{k}{800p}.
$$

researcher-0002:P9.1 [conditional; polished] Small-Component Corollary
DEPS=P1,P2,P3,P4,P5,P6,P7,P8,KTZ Lemma 3.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let

$$
\epsilon=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
$$

where $L$ is uniform among all affine lines of $\mathbb{F}_p^2$ and $x$ is uniform on $L$. Let $G_0$ be a graph supplied by KTZ Lemma 3.1 with minimum degree at least

$$
k_0=\left\lfloor\frac{\epsilon p}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Let $\Lambda\ge1$, and suppose $G_0$ has a connected component of line measure $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$. Put $C_*=2^{40}$ and assume

$$
\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000C_*\Lambda}\left(\frac{d}{p}\right)^{1/2}.
$$

Then there exists $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.
$$

researcher-0002:P10.1 [proved; polished] Balanced-Color Realization
DEPS=Chernoff bound
Let $p\ge103$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $K$ be an integer satisfying

$$
2\le K\le\frac{p}{100\log p}.
$$

There exist a function $f:\mathbb{F}_p^2\to\mathbb{F}_p$ and a constant polynomial $P_L$ on every affine line $L$ such that, with

$$
\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L)=|\{x\in L:P_L(x)=f(x)\}|
$$

and

$$
\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(x)=|\{L\ni x:P_L(x)=f(x)\}|,
$$

one has, for every affine line $L$ and every point $x$,

$$
\frac{p}{2K}\le\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L)\le\frac{2p}{K},
$$

$$
\frac{p+1}{2K}\le\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(x)\le\frac{2(p+1)}{K}.
$$

The test agreement satisfies

$$
\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K.
$$

researcher-0002:P11.1 [proved; polished] Covering Lower Bound
DEPS=P10
Let $f$ and the line table be as in P10. For a set $R$ of affine lines, define

$$
S_R=\{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2:\exists\,M\in R\text{ with }x\in M\text{ and }P_M(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose that every affine line $L$ satisfies

$$
\left|S_R\cap\{x\in L:P_L(x)=f(x)\}\right|
\ge\frac12\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L).
$$

Then

$$
|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon}.
$$

Moreover, there exists a constant polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac1K\ge\frac{\epsilon}{2}.
$$

researcher-0003:P1.1 [proved; polished] Weighted Sylvester degree
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and $d\ge1$. Give $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ the weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with weighted degrees $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, respectively, where at least one of $r,s$ is positive. Then $$\deg_{X,Y}\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)\le su+rv-drs=\frac{uv}{d}-\frac{(u-dr)(v-ds)}{d}\le\frac{uv}{d}.$$

researcher-0003:P2.1 [proved; polished] Common graph-line count
DEPS=researcher-0003:P1.1
Under the hypotheses of the weighted Sylvester-degree lemma, let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$. For every $L\in\mathcal T$, let $q_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ have degree at most $d$ and suppose $$U(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0.$$ Then $$|\mathcal T|\le\left\lfloor\frac{uv}{d}\right\rfloor.$$

researcher-0003:P3.1 [proved; polished] Weighted graph interpolation
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $1\le d\le D<p$. Let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$, and assign to each $L\in\mathcal R$ a polynomial $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$. Define $$N_d(D)=\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dk+2}{2}.$$ If $n>D$ and $N_d(D)>n(D+1)$, then there exists a nonzero $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0\qquad(L\in\mathcal R),$$ and $A$ depends on $Z$ with $A_Z\ne0$.

researcher-0003:P4.1 [proved; polished] Primitive squarefree reduction
DEPS=researcher-0003:P3.1
Let $p,d,D,\mathcal R$, and $\{P_L\}_{L\in\mathcal R}$ satisfy the hypotheses of the weighted graph-interpolation lemma. Among the nonzero polynomials satisfying all graph identities, let $A$ have minimum weighted degree. Then $A$ is $Z$-dependent and squarefree. If $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content of $A$ as a polynomial in $Z$, then $B$ is primitive in $Z$ and squarefree. Moreover, for every $L\in\mathcal R$ with $H(L(t))\not\equiv0$, $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$

researcher-0003:P5.1 [proved; polished] Vertical separability
DEPS=researcher-0003:P4.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $1\le d\le D<p$. Give $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ the weights $(1,1,d)$. If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is $Z$-dependent, squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in $Z$, and has weighted degree at most $D$, then $$\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$

researcher-0003:P6.1 [proved; polished] Vertical ramification cleanup
DEPS=researcher-0003:P2.1,researcher-0003:P5.1
Let $p$ be prime and $1\le d\le D<p$. Let $A=HB\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ have weighted degree at most $D$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the $Z$-content and $B$ is $Z$-dependent, primitive, and squarefree. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines, each carrying $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$, such that $A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Call $L$ content-trivial when $H(L(t))\equiv0$ and identically ramified when $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Then at most $$\left\lfloor\frac{D^2}{d}-D\right\rfloor$$ lines in $\mathcal T$ are content-trivial or identically ramified. Every other $L\in\mathcal T$ contains at most $D-d$ values $t\in\mathbb F_p$ satisfying $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))=0$. Under uniform affine point-line sampling, deleting all these lines and points loses incidence mass at most $$\frac{D^2/d-D}{p(p+1)}+\frac{D-d}{p}.$$

researcher-0003:P7.1 [proved; polished] Component-sensitive lifting
DEPS=researcher-0003:P5.1
Let $B$ be a primitive squarefree polynomial as in the vertical-separability lemma. Suppose $(\mathbf b,\alpha)\in\mathbb F_p^2\times\mathbb F_p$ satisfies $B(\mathbf b,\alpha)=0$ and $B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$. Then there is a unique irreducible factor $C$ of $B$ vanishing at $(\mathbf b,\alpha)$. Set $\delta_C=\operatorname{wdeg}C$. Suppose more than $\delta_C$ distinct affine lines through $\mathbf b$, parameterized by $L(0)=\mathbf b$, carry polynomials $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfying $\deg P_L\le d$, $P_L(0)=\alpha$, and $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$$ on every supplied line and $$C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0.$$ Moreover, $C$ is associated to $Z-Q$.

researcher-0003:P8.1 [conditional; polished] KTZ chain substitution
DEPS=researcher-0003:P3.1,researcher-0003:P6.1,KTZ Lemmas 3.1–4.3 and 6.1–6.3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d\le p-1$, and let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and degree-at-most-$d$ line labels $\{P_L\}$ have uniform point-line incidence agreement $\varepsilon$. Assume the hypotheses and conclusions of KTZ Lemmas 3.1–4.3 and 6.1–6.3 apply, with the weighted graph interpolation and vertical ramification cleanup used at their corresponding stages. If $\varepsilon\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}$ for a sufficiently large absolute constant $C$, then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{\varepsilon}{8000}.$$

researcher-0003:P9.1 [proved; polished] Uniform multiplicity barrier
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $D\ge d\ge101$. Let $W_D$ be the space of polynomials in $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of $(1,1,d)$-weighted degree at most $D$. Suppose ambient Hasse multiplicity $s\ge1$ is imposed at $M$ lifted points and existence is certified by $$M\binom{s+2}{3}<\dim W_D.$$ If multiplicity root counting on a target graph containing $k$ interpolated points requires $sk>D$, then $$k^3>\frac{Md}{12}.$$ In particular, if $M\ge\rho p^2$ and $k\le K\varepsilon p$, then $$\varepsilon^3>\frac{\rho}{12K^3}\frac{d}{p}.$$

researcher-0003:P10.1 [refuted; polished] Higher-Hasse lifting claim
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and $1\le d\le D<p$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive, irreducible, squarefree, and of $(1,1,d)$-weighted degree at most $D$, with $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. Let $(\mathbf b,\alpha)$ satisfy $B(\mathbf b,\alpha)=B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)=0$ and $\partial_Z^{[j]}B(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$ for some $j\ge2$. If more than $D$ distinct affine lines through $\mathbf b$, parameterized by $L(0)=\mathbf b$, carry polynomials $P_L$ with $\deg P_L\le d$, $P_L(0)=\alpha$, and $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$ on all supplied lines and $B(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$.

researcher-0003:P11.1 [proved; polished] Uniform-sampling exponent ceiling
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d\le p-1$, and set $N=\binom{d+2}{2}$. If $$N\log p<(4\log4-3)p,$$ then there exist a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and degree-at-most-$d$ labels on every affine line such that their uniform local agreement $\varepsilon$ satisfies $$\varepsilon\ge\frac{d+1}{p},$$ while every $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]<\frac4p.$$ Consequently, for every fixed $\alpha>1$ and fixed positive constants $C,c$, there are arbitrarily large such instances with $\varepsilon\ge C(d/p)^\alpha$ but global agreement below $c\varepsilon$ for every total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial.

researcher-0003:P12.1 [refuted; polished] Algebraic exponent-improvement claim
DEPS=researcher-0003:P6.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d\le p-1$, let $0<\varepsilon\le1$, and let $d\le D<p$ satisfy $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\varepsilon})$. Then there exists an exponent $\alpha>1/3$ such that the hypothesis $\varepsilon\gtrsim(d/p)^\alpha$ suffices, using the vertical ramification cleanup alone, to make its surviving loss $O(\varepsilon)$.

researcher-0004:P1.1 [proved; polished] Adapted normal coordinates
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $M(t)=b_M+tv_M$ be an affine line in $\mathbb F_p^2$ with a polynomial $P_M\in\mathbb F_p[T]$ satisfying $\deg P_M\le d$. Choose $u_M\in\mathbb F_p^2$ such that $(v_M,u_M)$ is a basis. Give $T$ and $S$ weight $1$ and $W$ weight $d$. Then

$$
(X,Y)=b_M+Tv_M+Su_M,\qquad Z=P_M(T)+W
$$

defines a filtered polynomial isomorphism between $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ and $\mathbb F_p[T,S,W]$. Under this isomorphism, the defining ideal $I_M$ of the lifted graph of $P_M$ is $(S,W)$.

researcher-0004:P2.1 [proved; polished] Normal-jet codimension
DEPS=P1
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $V_D\subseteq\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be the space of polynomials of weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$. For a lifted line-graph ideal $I_M$, define

$$
C_s(D,d)=\sum_{\substack{a,b\ge 0\\a+b<s}}\max\{D-a-db+1,0\}.
$$

Then $V_D\cap I_M^s$ has codimension exactly $C_s(D,d)$ in $V_D$. If $\mathcal R$ consists of $n$ distinct lifted line graphs, then the simultaneous conditions $A\in I_M^s$ for all $M\in\mathcal R$ impose at most $nC_s(D,d)$ homogeneous linear equations on $V_D$.

researcher-0004:P3.1 [proved; polished] Jet interpolation
DEPS=P2
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Define

$$
N_d(D)=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dj+2}{2}.
$$

If $N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d)$, then there exists a nonzero polynomial $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$.

researcher-0004:P4.1 [proved; polished] Line-factor divisibility
DEPS=P1,P3
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines. Let $A\ne 0$ have weighted degree at most $D$ and satisfy $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. For each $M\in\mathcal R$, let $\ell_M$ be a nonzero affine-linear equation defining $M$. If $A$ is independent of $Z$, then

$$
\prod_{M\in\mathcal R}\ell_M^s\mid A
$$

and $D\ge sn$. Consequently, if $D<sn$, then $A$ depends on $Z$.

researcher-0004:P4.2 [proved; polished] Primitive degree cost
DEPS=P1,P3
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines. Let $A\ne0$ have weighted degree at most $D$ and satisfy $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. If $n>D$, then

$$
\deg_Z A\ge s
$$

and $D\ge sd$.

researcher-0004:P4.3 [proved; polished] Formal derivative
DEPS=P1,P3
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $d\le D<p$. If a polynomial $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ has weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$ and depends on $Z$, then its formal derivative satisfies

$$
A_Z=\frac{\partial A}{\partial Z}\ne0.
$$

researcher-0004:P5.1 [proved; polished] Transverse root multiplicity
DEPS=P1
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $M$ and $L$ be distinct affine lines with parameterizations $M(r)$ and $L(t)$. Let $P_M$ and $P_L$ have degree at most $d$. Suppose that

$$
L(t_0)=M(r_0),\qquad P_L(t_0)=P_M(r_0).
$$

If $A$ has weighted degree at most $D$ and $A\in I_M^s$, then

$$
(t-t_0)^s\mid A(L(t),P_L(t)).
$$

researcher-0004:P6.1 [proved; polished] Target-line transfer
DEPS=P5
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Let $A$ have weighted degree at most $D$ and satisfy $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. Let $L$ be an affine line with a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Suppose there are distinct parameters $t_1,\ldots,t_h$ and, for each $i$, a line $M_i\in\mathcal R$ with $M_i\ne L$ and a parameter $r_i$ such that

$$
L(t_i)=M_i(r_i),\qquad P_L(t_i)=P_{M_i}(r_i).
$$

If $sh>D$, then

$$
A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

researcher-0004:P7.1 [proved; polished] Explicit degree choice
DEPS=P2,P3,P6
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $n\ge1$ and $s\ge1$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Define

$$
D_0=\left\lfloor4\sqrt{dns(s+1)}\right\rfloor+1.
$$

If $d\le D_0<p$, then there exists a nonzero polynomial $A$ of weighted degree at most $D_0$ satisfying $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. If, in addition, $D_0<sh$, then every target line having $h$ distinct compatible intersections with members of $\mathcal R$ satisfies

$$
A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

researcher-0004:P8.1 [proved; polished] Rank-blind barrier
DEPS=P3,P4,P6
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Define

$$
N_d(D)=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dj+2}{2},\qquad C_s(D,d)=\sum_{\substack{a,b\ge0\\a+b<s}}\max\{D-a-db+1,0\}.
$$

Suppose that

$$
n>D,\qquad N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d),\qquad sh>D.
$$

Then

$$
h^2>\frac{dn}{12}.
$$

If, along a parameter family with $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$, one additionally has $n=\Theta(p/\varepsilon)$ and $h=\Theta(\varepsilon p)$, then

$$
\varepsilon^3=\Omega\!\left(\frac{d}{p}\right).
$$

researcher-0005:[P1].1 [proved; polished] Hasse Height
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Assign weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $C=\sum_{k\ge0}c_k(X,Y)Z^k\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent, and define

$$e:=\min\{v_p(k):k>0,\ c_k\ne0\},\qquad s:=p^e.$$

For the Hasse derivatives determined by

$$C(X,Y,Z+T)=\sum_{r\ge0}\partial_Z^{[r]}C(X,Y,Z)T^r,$$

one has $\partial_Z^{[r]}C=0$ for every $0<r<s$, while $\partial_Z^{[s]}C\ne0$, $\gcd(C,\partial_Z^{[s]}C)=1$, and $\operatorname{wdeg}C\ge ds$.

researcher-0005:[P2].1 [proved; polished] Vertical Multiplicity
DEPS=[P1]
Let $B=c\prod_{i=1}^r C_i\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$, where $c\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ and the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent polynomials with Hasse heights $s_i$. If an affine-line parametrization $L(t)$ and a polynomial $q(t)\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfy

$$C_i(L(t),q(t))\equiv0,$$

then

$$(Z-q(t))^{s_i}\mid C_i(L(t),Z)$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t,Z]$. Moreover, $\gcd(B,B_Z)$ has exactly the irreducible factors $C_i$ for which $s_i>1$.

researcher-0005:[P3].1 [proved; polished] Weighted Resultants
DEPS=
Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with

$$u=\operatorname{wdeg}U,\quad v=\operatorname{wdeg}V,\quad r=\deg_ZU,\quad s=\deg_ZV,$$

under the weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a set of distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$, each equipped with one polynomial $q_L$ of degree at most $d$, such that

$$U(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t]$. Then

$$|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs\le\frac{uv}{d}.$$

researcher-0005:[P4].1 [proved; polished] Hasse Strata
DEPS=[P1],[P3]
Let $s$ be a fixed $p$-power, and let

$$G_s:=\prod_{i:s_i=s}C_i,
\qquad \Delta_s:=\operatorname{wdeg}G_s,
\qquad r_s:=\deg_ZG_s$$

for a nonempty collection of distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent factors of Hasse height $s$. For any family of affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels, the number of lines satisfying both formal identities

$$G_s(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad
\partial_Z^{[s]}G_s(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

is at most

$$(2r_s-s)\Delta_s-dr_s^2.$$

researcher-0005:[P5].1 [proved; polished] Content Removal
DEPS=
Let

$$A(X,Y,Z)=H(X,Y)B(X,Y,Z),
\qquad h:=\deg H,$$

where $H\ne0$ and $B=c\prod_{i=1}^rC_i$ with $c\in\mathbb F_p^\times$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels satisfying

$$A(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t]$. At most $h$ lines in $\mathcal T$ satisfy $H(L(t))\equiv0$. Every other line satisfies $B(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$ and $C_i(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$ for at least one $i$.

researcher-0005:[P6].1 [proved; polished] Exceptional Lines
DEPS=[P3],[P5]
Let

$$B=c\prod_{i=1}^rC_i,
\qquad \delta_i:=\operatorname{wdeg}C_i,
\qquad \Delta:=\sum_i\delta_i,$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent factors, and let

$$\iota:=\operatorname{wdeg}\gcd(B,B_Z)=\sum_{C_{i,Z}=0}\delta_i.$$

For $C_{i,Z}\ne0$, set $D_iC_i:=C_{i,Z}$ and $\omega_i:=d$. For $C_{i,Z}=0$, at least one member of $\{C_{i,X},C_{i,Y}\}$ is nonzero; choose one as $D_iC_i$ and set $\omega_i:=1$.

Among the non-content graph lines supplied by [P5], call a line exceptional if either at least two distinct factors vanish identically on it, or exactly one factor $C_i$ vanishes identically and $D_iC_i$ also vanishes identically. The number $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$ of exceptional lines satisfies

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\sum_i\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i(\delta_i-\omega_i)}{d}\right\rfloor
+
\sum_{i<j}\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i\delta_j}{d}\right\rfloor$$

and

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^2-d\Delta+(d-1)\iota}{d}\right\rfloor.$$

researcher-0005:[P7].1 [proved; polished] Root Deletion
DEPS=[P6]
Retain the notation of [P6], and suppose at most $h$ content lines and $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$ exceptional lines have been removed. On each remaining line of unique type $i$, delete every parameter value at which $D_iC_i$ or any factor $C_j$ with $j\ne i$ vanishes. The number deleted from that line is at most

$$\min\{p,\Delta-\omega_i\}.$$

Thus a separable-type line loses at most $\min\{p,\Delta-d\}$ points, and an inseparable-type line loses at most $\min\{p,\Delta-1\}$ points. If $\sigma_{\mathrm{sep}}$ and $\sigma_{\mathrm{ins}}$ are the respective numbers of remaining separable- and inseparable-type lines divided by $p(p+1)$, the deleted fraction of the ambient incidence space is at most

$$\frac{h+N_{\mathrm{exc}}}{p(p+1)}
+\sigma_{\mathrm{sep}}\min\left\{1,\frac{\Delta-d}{p}\right\}
+\sigma_{\mathrm{ins}}\min\left\{1,\frac{\Delta-1}{p}\right\}.$$

Deleting all remaining point vertices of degree at most $1$ adds at most $1/(p+1)$ to this fraction.

researcher-0005:[P8].1 [proved; polished] Simple Intersections
DEPS=[P7]
In the incidence system retained after [P7], let $(x,y,z)\in\mathbb F_p^3$ be incident to at least two distinct retained graph lines. Then

$$B(x,y,z)=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
B_Z(x,y,z)\ne0.$$

researcher-0005:[P9].1 [proved; polished] Automatic Separability
DEPS=[P1],[P6],[P7],[P8]
Let $B=c\prod_iC_i$ be squarefree and $Z$-dependent, with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}B$ and $\iota=\operatorname{wdeg}\gcd(B,B_Z)$. If

$$\Delta<pd,$$

then

$$\iota=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$

If additionally $A=HB$, $h=\deg H$, $a=h+\Delta$, and

$$a\le D<pd,$$

then the cleanup removes at most

$$\left\lfloor\frac{D^2}{d}-D\right\rfloor$$

content and exceptional lines in total and at most $D-d$ parameter values from every other line. Every retained point incident to at least two retained graph lines satisfies $B_Z\ne0$.

researcher-0005:[P10].1 [proved; polished] KTZ Parameter Insertion
DEPS=[P9],KTZ Theorem 1.1 and Lemmas 3.2–6.3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line labels $q_L$ have local agreement

$$\epsilon:=\Pr_{L\in\mathcal L,\,x\in L}[q_L(x)=f(x)].$$

Let $C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}$ be the absolute constant in the quoted KTZ theorem. If

$$\epsilon\ge C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3},$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$\frac{|\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x,y)=f(x,y)\}|}{p^2}\ge\frac{\epsilon}{8000}.$$

researcher-0005:[P11].1 [proved; polished] Inseparable Pencil
DEPS=[P1]
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Define

$$F(X,Y,Z):=Z^p-XY^{p-1}.$$

Then $F$ is irreducible and squarefree, $\operatorname{wdeg}F=pd$, $F_Z=0$, $\partial_Z^{[r]}F=0$ for $1\le r<p$, and $\partial_Z^{[p]}F=1$. For every $[a:b]\in\mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_p)$, set

$$L_{a,b}(t):=(at,bt),
\qquad
q_{a,b}(t):=ab^{p-1}t.$$

Then

$$F(L_{a,b}(t),Z)=(Z-q_{a,b}(t))^p.$$

Consequently, the multiplicity-weighted direction count is

$$p(p+1)>pd=\operatorname{wdeg}F,$$

but there is no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[[X,Y]]$ satisfying $F(X,Y,Q(X,Y))=0$.

researcher-0005:[P12].1 [refuted; polished] Soundness Counterexample Attempt
DEPS=[P11]
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Define

$$f(x,y):=xy^{p-1}.$$

Label every affine line other than the horizontal axis by the restriction of $X$, and label the horizontal axis by the constant polynomial $0$. Then this table and label family is a counterexample to affine line-versus-point soundness.

researcher-0005:[P13].1 [refuted; polished] Direction-Concentration Attempt
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $0\le k\le p+1$. Set $f=0$, label every line in $k$ complete direction classes by the constant polynomial $0$, and label every other affine line by the constant polynomial $1$. Then concentration of all accepted incidences in those $k$ direction classes obstructs global recovery by a total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial.

researcher-0006:P1.1 [proved; polished] Vertical separability
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in $Z$, and satisfies $\operatorname{wdeg}B<dp$, then $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.

researcher-0006:P2.1 [proved; polished] Weighted resultant bound
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with $u=\operatorname{wdeg}U$, $v=\operatorname{wdeg}V$, $r=\deg_ZU$, and $s=\deg_ZV$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines with parametrizations $L(t)$ and labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ such that $U(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ for every $L\in\mathcal T$. Then $|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs$.

researcher-0006:P3.1 [proved; polished] Frobenius purification
DEPS=P2
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent, with $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}C$, $r=\deg_ZC$, and exactly $k\ge2$ absolute factors. If $\mathcal T$ is a family of distinct affine $\mathbb F_p$-lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$ satisfying $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, then $|\mathcal T|\le(2r\delta-dr^2)/k^2\le\delta^2/(dk^2)\le\delta^2/(4d)$.

researcher-0006:P4.1 [proved; polished] Factor aggregation
DEPS=P3
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$ and satisfy $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine $\mathbb F_p$-lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels such that every $L\in\mathcal T$ is supported by an $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible, geometrically reducible factor of $B$. Then $|\mathcal T|\le D^2/(4d)$. If additionally $p>2d$, $\nu\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ is a nonsquare, and $H_0$ is a product of $d$ distinct affine-line equations, then $C=Z^2-\nu H_0^2$ is $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible with two absolute factors and supports exactly $d=(2d)^2/(4d)$ degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines.

researcher-0006:P5.1 [proved; polished] Smooth ramification sparsity
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$, let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and let $\mathcal T$ be a family of affine lines parametrized by $L(t)$ with labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Let $\mathcal E$ be the set of pairs $(L,\tau)$ satisfying $P_L(\tau)=f(L(\tau))$ and $B_Z(L(\tau),P_L(\tau))=0$. If every lift $(L(\tau),P_L(\tau))$ belonging to $\mathcal E$ is smooth on $B=0$, then $|\mathcal E|\le p^2$, and $|\mathcal E|/[p^2(p+1)]\le1/(p+1)$.

researcher-0006:P6.1 [proved; polished] Linear vertical regime
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $d\le D<2d$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, $Z$-dependent, and satisfy $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D$. Then $|\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:\exists z\in\mathbb F_p,\ B(x,y,z)=B_Z(x,y,z)=0\}|\le D(D-d)$, and there is no affine-line parametrization $L(t)$ with a degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $P(t)$ satisfying both $B(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$ and $B_Z(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$.

researcher-0006:P7.1 [proved; polished] Singular construction algebra
DEPS=P1,P6
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d\le p-2$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, and set $D=d+1$, $s=\lfloor(d+1)/2\rfloor$, and $e=d+1-2s\in\{0,1\}$. Choose distinct $c_1,\ldots,c_s\in\mathbb F_p$. If $e=1$, choose $b\in\mathbb F_p\setminus\{c_1,\ldots,c_s\}$, and define $H(Y)=\prod_{j=1}^s(Y-c_j)^2(Y-b)^e$ and $B(X,Y,Z)=XZ+H(Y)$, with the last factor omitted when $e=0$. Then $B$ is primitive in $Z$, squarefree, and absolutely irreducible, with $\operatorname{wdeg}B=D<p$, $B_Z=X$, and $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. Its normalized degree-one discriminant is $1$, while $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=X$, whose zero set is a smooth absolutely irreducible affine line. For every $1\le j\le s$, the point $(0,c_j,0)$ is singular on $B=0$.

researcher-0006:P8.1 [proved; polished] Explicit incidence table
DEPS=P7
Under the hypotheses and notation of [P7], for $1\le j\le s$ and $m\in\mathbb F_p$ define $L_{j,m}(t)=(t,c_j+mt)$ and $P_{j,m}(t)=-H(c_j+mt)/t$. Define $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ by $f(x,y)=-H(y)/x$ when $x\ne0$ and $f(0,y)=0$, and give every unselected affine line the constant label $0$. Then every $P_{j,m}$ is a polynomial of degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(L_{j,m}(t),P_{j,m}(t))\equiv0$, and every one of the $sp>D$ selected lines is fully accepted by $f$. Their uniform line measure is $s/(p+1)$, so the table's agreement is at least $s/(p+1)$. Moreover, $B_Z(L_{j,m}(t),P_{j,m}(t))=t$, so each selected line has exactly one ramified accepted parameter, and the normalized mass of the selected ramified incidences is $s/[p(p+1)]$.

researcher-0006:P9.1 [proved; polished] Interpolation minimality
DEPS=P8
Under the hypotheses and notation of [P8], there is no polynomial $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $Q(L_{j,m}(t))\equiv P_{j,m}(t)$ for every $1\le j\le s$ and $m\in\mathbb F_p$. Moreover, every nonzero $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ satisfying $A(L_{j,m}(t),P_{j,m}(t))\equiv0$ for all selected lines has weighted degree at least $D=d+1$. Consequently, $B=XZ+H(Y)$ is a minimum-weight graph interpolant for the selected graph lines.

researcher-0006:P10.1 [proved; polished] Monic stress test
DEPS=P5
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $2d\le D<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, set $r=D-d$, choose distinct $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in\mathbb F_p$, and define $A(X)=\prod_{i=1}^r(X-a_i)$ and $B_{\mathrm{sm}}(X,Y,Z)=Z^2+A(X)Z+Y$. Then $B_{\mathrm{sm}}$ is monic in $Z$, has weighted degree $D$, and is smooth and absolutely irreducible. Its $Z$-discriminant $\Delta_{\mathrm{sm}}(X,Y)=A(X)^2-4Y$ is also smooth and absolutely irreducible. The label $P_L(t)=0$ on the line $L(t)=(t,0)$ satisfies $B_{\mathrm{sm}}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and $(B_{\mathrm{sm}})_Z(L(t),P_L(t))=A(t)$ has exactly $D-d$ distinct roots.

researcher-0006:P11.1 [proved; polished] Dense singular pencil
DEPS=P1
Let $p$ be prime, let $d\ge101$, and assume $p>4d+1$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, set $D=2d$, choose distinct nonzero $a_1,\ldots,a_{d-1}\in\mathbb F_p$, and define $g(X)=\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}(X-a_i)$ and $B_{\mathrm{den}}(X,Y,Z)=Z^2-XYg(X)^2$. Then $B_{\mathrm{den}}$ has weighted degree $D$ and is absolutely irreducible. For every nonzero square $s\in\mathbb F_p$, choose $c_s\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ with $c_s^2=s$ and set $L_s(t)=(t,st)$ and $P_s(t)=c_stg(t)$. Each $P_s$ has degree $d$ and satisfies $B_{\mathrm{den}}(L_s(t),P_s(t))\equiv0$. There are $(p-1)/2>D$ such graph lines. On every one, $(B_{\mathrm{den}})_Z$ vanishes at exactly the $d=D-d$ parameters $0,a_1,\ldots,a_{d-1}$, and every corresponding lift is singular on $B_{\mathrm{den}}=0$. There is no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ satisfying $B_{\mathrm{den}}(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$.

researcher-0006:P12.1 [refuted; polished] Simplicity substitutes
DEPS=P7,P8,P9,P10,P11
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. The following assertions hold:

1. If an absolutely irreducible graph interpolant of weighted degree at most $D<p$ supports more than $D$ distinct affine graph lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels, then it has a polynomial root $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$, without any simple-common-lift hypothesis.

2. Smooth absolute irreducibility of the raw-resultant curve $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=0$ controls the number of graph directions meeting a ramified lifted point.

3. If $2d\le D<p$, a graph interpolant is monic in $Z$, and its $Z$-discriminant is smooth and absolutely irreducible, then every degree-at-most-$d$ graph line contains $o(D-d)$ parameters at which $B_Z$ vanishes.

researcher-0006:P13.1 [conditional; polished] Singular deletion scale
DEPS=P3,P5,P12,Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $0<\epsilon\le1$. Suppose the interpolation weight satisfies $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$. Suppose a deletion argument assigns supported graph lines to fixed $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible factors, deletes graph lines supported on geometrically reducible factors using [P3], applies [P5] only to ramified lifts known to be smooth on their assigned factor, and charges every remaining singular ramified incidence by the per-line budget $D/p$. Then the normalized purification loss is $O(1/(\epsilon p))$, the normalized smooth-ramification loss for each fixed assigned factor is at most $1/(p+1)$, and the singular-ramification loss is $O(D/p)=O(\sqrt{d/(\epsilon p)})$. Requiring the last loss to be $O(\epsilon)$ imposes $\epsilon^3=\Omega(d/p)$.

researcher-0007:P1.1 [proved; polished] Factor separability
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, and write

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i,
\qquad c\in\mathbb F_p^\times,
$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible polynomials. For

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\qquad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
$$

every $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ satisfies

$$
r_i\ge1
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1.
$$

researcher-0007:P2.1 [proved; polished] Weighted resultant degree
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let

$$
U=\sum_{a=0}^{r}u_a(X,Y)Z^a,
\qquad
V=\sum_{b=0}^{s}v_b(X,Y)Z^b
$$

be nonzero polynomials in $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ satisfying

$$
\deg_Z(U)=r,
\qquad
\deg_Z(V)=s,
\qquad
\operatorname{wdeg}(U)=u,
\qquad
\operatorname{wdeg}(V)=v.
$$

With the convention $\deg_{X,Y}(0)=-\infty$,

$$
\deg_{X,Y}\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)\le su+rv-drs.
$$

researcher-0007:P3.1 [proved; polished] Exceptional graph lines
DEPS=researcher-0007:P1.1,researcher-0007:P2.1
Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P1.1. Fix $i$ and put

$$
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
\qquad
R_i=\operatorname{Res}_Z(C_i,C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T_i$ be a family indexed by distinct affine lines with equations $\ell_L(X,Y)=0$. For each $L\in\mathcal T_i$, let $L(t)=(x_L(t),y_L(t))$ be an affine-linear parametrization and let $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfy $\deg P_L\le d$ and

$$
C_i(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

Define

$$
\mathcal E_i=
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
$$

and, for $L\in\mathcal E_i$,

$$
\nu_L=\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_L}(R_i).
$$

Then

$$
R_i\ne0,
\qquad
\deg_{X,Y}(R_i)\le\Xi_i,
\qquad
\sum_{L\in\mathcal E_i}\nu_L\le\Xi_i.
$$

In particular,

$$
|\mathcal E_i|\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}.
$$

researcher-0007:P4.1 [proved; polished] Finite polar intersections
DEPS=researcher-0007:P1.1
Let the objects satisfy the hypotheses of researcher-0007:P3.1. For $L\in\mathcal T_i\setminus\mathcal E_i$, set

$$
G_{i,L}(t)=C_{i,Z}(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t)).
$$

Then $G_{i,L}\ne0$ and

$$
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb F_p}
\operatorname{ord}_{t=\alpha}G_{i,L}(t)
\le\gamma_i.
$$

researcher-0007:P5.1 [proved; polished] Factorwise cleanup bounds
DEPS=researcher-0007:P3.1,researcher-0007:P4.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, with

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i.
$$

For each $i$, put

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\quad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
\quad
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T=\bigsqcup_i\mathcal T_i$ be a disjoint assignment of distinct affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$, satisfying

$$
C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\qquad(L\in\mathcal T_i).
$$

For each $i$, let

$$
e_i=
\left|
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
\right|.
$$

For a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, let $i(L)=i$ when $L\in\mathcal T_i$, and define

$$
\mathcal R=
\left\{(L,t):
\begin{array}{l}
L\in\mathcal T,\ t\in\mathbb F_p,\\
P_L(t)=f(L(t)),\\
C_{i(L),Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0
\end{array}
\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\},
$$

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right),
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\sum_i\frac{\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}
+
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.
$$

Moreover,

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i\le(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2
\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
$$

If

$$
\tau_i=\frac{|\mathcal T_i|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_i\tau_i>0,
\qquad
\bar\delta=\frac{\sum_i\tau_i\delta_i}{\tau},
$$

then

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\frac{\delta^2/d-\delta}{p(p+1)}
+
\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$

researcher-0007:P6.1 [conditional; polished] Cleaned factor component
DEPS=researcher-0007:P1.1,researcher-0007:P5.1,KTZ Lemmas 2.1, 2.7, and 6.1–6.3
Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P5.1, and fix $i$. Let $G=(\mathcal X,T',E')$ be a connected bipartite graph with $\mathcal X\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2$, $T'\subseteq\mathcal T_i$, and

$$
E'\subseteq
\{(x,L)\in\mathcal X\times T':x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose $G$ has minimum degree at least the integer $k$, and suppose

$$
C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0
\qquad((x,L)\in E').
$$

If

$$
k>\delta_i
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}\le\frac{k}{4p},
$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
Q(x)=f(x)
\qquad(x\in\mathcal X)
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\{x\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\}|}{p^2}
\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$

researcher-0007:P7.1 [proved; polished] Strip construction
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, and let $d,D$ be integers satisfying

$$
101\le d,
\qquad
2d\le D<p.
$$

Put $e=D-d$, choose $S\subseteq\mathbb F_p$ with $|S|=e$, and define

$$
H(X)=\prod_{a\in S}(X-a),
\qquad
B=Z(Z-H(X)).
$$

Define $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ by

$$
f(x,y)=
\begin{cases}
0,&x\in S,\\
1,&x\notin S,
\end{cases}
$$

and assign the constant label $P_L=0$ to every nonvertical affine line and the constant label $P_L=2$ to every vertical affine line. Then $B$ is primitive in $Z$, squarefree, and has weighted degree $D$. Every nonvertical line has exactly $e$ accepted points and satisfies

$$
B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
$$

while every vertical line has no accepted points. The test agreement is

$$
\varepsilon=\frac{e}{p+1}=\frac{D-d}{p+1}.
$$

researcher-0007:P8.1 [proved; polished] Polar-loss saturation
DEPS=researcher-0007:P7.1
Let $p,d,D,e,S,H,B,f$ and the line labels be as in researcher-0007:P7.1, and put

$$
\tau=\frac{p}{p+1}.
$$

On every nonvertical zero-labelled graph, $B_Z(L(t),0)$ has exactly $D-d$ simple roots, and these roots are exactly the accepted parameters. The whole-relation ramified-incidence measure is

$$
\frac{D-d}{p+1}
=\frac{\tau(D-d)}p
=\varepsilon.
$$

Assigning every supported graph to the factor $C=Z$ gives $C_Z=1$ and factorwise polar loss $0$. The polynomial $Q=0$ has total degree $0$ and global agreement

$$
\frac{D-d}{p}>\varepsilon.
$$

researcher-0007:P9.1 [proved; polished] Cubic parameter scale
DEPS=researcher-0007:P7.1,researcher-0007:P8.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $d$ be an integer satisfying

$$
101\le d
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
p\ge8d.
$$

Set

$$
e=\left\lceil(dp^2)^{1/3}\right\rceil,
\qquad
D=d+e,
\qquad
\varepsilon=\frac e{p+1}.
$$

Then

$$
e\ge4d,
\qquad
e\le\frac p2+1,
\qquad
2d\le D<p,
$$

and

$$
\frac12\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3}
\le\varepsilon
\le2\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3}.
$$

For the construction of researcher-0007:P7.1 and researcher-0007:P8.1,

$$
D=\Theta(\varepsilon p)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
D=\Theta\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{dp}{\varepsilon}}\right).
$$

researcher-0007:P10.1 [refuted; polished] Minimum-interpolant saturation
DEPS=researcher-0007:P7.1,researcher-0007:P8.1
Under the construction of researcher-0007:P7.1 and researcher-0007:P8.1, the interpolant selected by KTZ's minimum-weight rule incurs the whole-relation polar loss

$$
\frac{\tau(D-d)}p.
$$

researcher-0007:P11.1 [refuted; polished] Inseparability attempt
DEPS=researcher-0007:P1.1
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with $X,Y,Z$ carrying weights $1,1,d$. Under the campaign requirement that a relation have weighted degree $\delta<p$, the polynomial

$$
B=Z^p-X
$$

is an inseparability counterexample to the factorwise theorem.

researcher-0007:P11.2 [refuted; polished] Direction-concentration attempt
DEPS=researcher-0007:P1.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $1\le k\le p+1$. An instance with $f=0$ whose correct labels are concentrated in exactly $k$ direction classes refutes the factorwise theorem in the campaign regime.

researcher-0008:P1.1 [proved; polished] Affine incidence decomposition
DEPS=Hall's marriage theorem, proved in the step by regular degree counting
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, let $\mathcal P=\mathbb F_p^2$, let $\mathcal L$ be the set of affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$, and let

$$E=\{(x,L)\in\mathcal P\times\mathcal L:x\in L\}.$$

There is a partition of $E$ into $p+1$ color classes such that every point is incident to exactly one edge of each color, every affine line is incident to exactly one edge of all but one color and to no edge of the remaining color, and every color is omitted by exactly $p$ affine lines.

researcher-0008:P2.1 [proved; polished] Regular incidence carrier
DEPS=P1,Hall's marriage theorem
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. The affine point-line incidence graph over $\mathbb F_p^2$ has a spanning subgraph in which every affine line has degree $d$, exactly $pd$ points have degree $d+1$, and the remaining $p(p-d)$ points have degree $d$.

researcher-0008:P3.1 [proved; polished] Polynomial carrier realization
DEPS=P2
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $H$ be the spanning incidence subgraph supplied by P2. For every affine line $L$, fix an affine bijection $t\mapsto L(t)$ from $\mathbb F_p$ onto $L$. There exist a point table $f\colon\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\deg P_L=d$ such that

$$P_L(t)=f(L(t))\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad (L(t),L)\in E(H).$$

The resulting table has agreement $\epsilon=d/p$, every affine line has exactly $d$ accepted points, every point has accepted degree $d$ or $d+1$, exactly $pd$ points have accepted degree $d+1$, every affine line supports acceptance, and the total-degree-$0$ polynomial $Q=0$ agrees with $f$ everywhere.

researcher-0008:P4.1 [proved; polished] Fractional reaching lower bound
DEPS=P2,P3
For the table constructed in P3, write $\mathcal L$ for the affine lines, $A(x,L)\in\{0,1\}$ for the acceptance indicator,

$$N_E(L)=\{x\in L:A(x,L)=1\},\qquad a_x=\sum_{L\ni x}A(x,L).$$

Let $0<\theta\le1$, let $\lambda=(\lambda_M)_{M\in\mathcal L}$ be nonnegative line weights, and define

$$u_{x,L}(\lambda)=\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ni x\\M\ne L}}A(x,M)\lambda_M\right\}$$

for every accepted incidence $(x,L)$. If

$$\sum_{x\in N_E(L)}u_{x,L}(\lambda)\ge\theta\lvert N_E(L)\rvert$$

for every $L\in\mathcal L$, then

$$\sum_{M\in\mathcal L}\lambda_M\ge\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}.$$

The same conclusion holds when every $\lambda_M$ belongs to $\{0,1\}$.

researcher-0008:P5.1 [proved; polished] Uniform reaching weights
DEPS=P2,P3
For the table constructed in P3 and every $0<\theta\le1$, assign every affine line the weight

$$\lambda_M=\frac{\theta}{d-1}.$$

These weights are $\theta$-reaching through other accepted lines and have total weight

$$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

If $\operatorname{OPT}_\theta$ is the infimum of the total weight over all nonnegative $\theta$-reaching weights, then

$$\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1},$$

and the multiplicative gap between this upper bound and the lower-bound value $\theta p(p+1)/(d+1)$ is

$$\frac{d+1}{d-1}\le\frac{102}{100}.$$

researcher-0008:P6.1 [proved; polished] Two-line collision identity
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and consider any valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ with line-label degree at most $d$. Let $A(x,L)$ be its acceptance indicator, let

$$\epsilon=\frac{\sum_{x,L\ni x}A(x,L)}{p^2(p+1)},\qquad a_x=\sum_{L\ni x}A(x,L),$$

and let $\Gamma_2$ be the probability that a uniformly chosen ordered pair of distinct lines through a uniformly chosen point are both accepted there. Then

$$\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}p+\frac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$

researcher-0008:P7.1 [proved; polished] Direction-class equality
DEPS=P6
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $1\le k\le p+1$ be an integer. There is a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ with degree-at-most-$d$ labels such that every point has accepted degree $k$,

$$\epsilon=\frac{k}{p+1},\qquad \operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)=0,
\qquad \Gamma_2=\frac{k(k-1)}{p(p+1)}.$$

The collision lower bound in P6 is attained, and the total-degree-$0$ polynomial $Q=0$ has global agreement $1$.

researcher-0008:P8.1 [proved; polished] KTZ exponent reconstruction
DEPS=Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Revision 1, Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1–4.3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $\epsilon>0$. Suppose a reaching family $R$ and a weighted interpolation degree $D$ satisfy

$$\lvert R\rvert=O\left(\frac p\epsilon\right),\qquad D=O\left(\sqrt{d\lvert R\rvert}\right)=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{dp}{\epsilon}}\right),$$

and suppose the distinct-root and lifting budget imposes $D=O(\epsilon p)$. Then the resulting parameter threshold is

$$\epsilon^3=\Omega\left(\frac dp\right).$$

researcher-0008:P9.1 [proved; polished] Vinh recovery barrier
DEPS=Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Revision 1, Lemmas 2.1 and 6.2
Fix $C>0$ and $\alpha>1/2$, set $d=101$, and let $p>101$ tend to infinity through primes. If

$$\epsilon=C\left(\frac{101}{p}\right)^\alpha,$$

then $\epsilon\sqrt p\to0$, and the absorption condition

$$(p+1)^{-1/2}\le\frac{\epsilon}{8000}$$

fails for all sufficiently large $p$. The KTZ/Vinh component-recovery step does not yield its linear global-agreement conclusion in this regime.

researcher-0008:P10.1 [proved; polished] Balanced incidence diagnostic
DEPS=Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorems 3 and 4
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, let $P\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2$ contain $N$ points, and let $T$ be a set of $N$ distinct affine lines. Suppose their incidence graph has minimum degree at least $k$ and satisfies the hypotheses of Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 3,

$$\lvert P\rvert^{7/8}<\lvert T\rvert<\lvert P\rvert^{8/7},\qquad \lvert P\rvert^{-2}\lvert T\rvert^{13}\ll p^{15}.$$

Then

$$N\gg k^{15/7}.$$

For this lower-bound scale to reach $N=\Omega(kp)$, one must have $k\gtrsim p^{7/8}$ and $N\gtrsim p^{15/8}$, which is outside the characteristic range $N\ll p^{15/11}$.

researcher-0008:P10.2 [proved; polished] Cartesian incidence diagnostic
DEPS=Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorems 3 and 4
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, let $P=A\times B\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2$ satisfy $\lvert A\rvert=\lvert B\rvert=\sqrt N$, and let $T$ be a set of $N$ distinct affine lines. Suppose the incidence graph has minimum degree at least $k$, the linear term in the incidence estimate is absorbed, and the hypotheses of Stevens–de Zeeuw Theorem 4 hold:

$$\lvert A\rvert\le\lvert B\rvert,
\qquad \lvert A\rvert\lvert B\rvert^2\le\lvert T\rvert^3,
\qquad \lvert A\rvert\lvert T\rvert\ll p^2.$$

Then

$$N\gg k^{8/3}.$$

For this lower-bound scale to reach $N=\Omega(kp)$, one must have $k\gtrsim p^{3/5}$ and $N\gtrsim p^{8/5}$, which is outside the characteristic range $N\ll p^{4/3}$.

researcher-0008:P11.1 [refuted; polished] Incidence-only shortcut
DEPS=P4,P5,P6,P7
For every valid affine line-versus-point table of acceptance density $\epsilon$, acceptance density alone yields either compatible distinct-line-pair density $\omega(\epsilon^2)$ or a nonnegative line-weight family of total weight $o(p/\epsilon)$ that reaches a fixed positive fraction of the accepted points of every accepted line through other accepted lines.

researcher-0009:P1.1 [proved; polished] Distinct-star counts
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have agreement $\epsilon$, accepted point degrees $a_x$, and accepted line degrees $b_L$. For integers $u,s\ge0$, write $(u)_0=1$ and, for $s\ge1$, $(u)_s=u(u-1)\cdots(u-s+1)$. For $0\le s\le p+1$, let $\Gamma_s^P$ be the probability that, after choosing a uniform point and an ordered $s$-tuple of distinct incident lines uniformly, all sampled incidences are accepted. For $0\le s\le p$, define $\Gamma_s^L$ analogously using a uniform line and an ordered $s$-tuple of distinct points on it. Then

$$\Gamma_s^P=\frac{\mathbb E_x[(a_x)_s]}{(p+1)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$

and

$$\Gamma_s^L=\frac{\mathbb E_L[(b_L)_s]}{(p)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p).$$

researcher-0009:P2.1 [proved; polished] Degree-moment envelopes
DEPS=P1
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have agreement $\epsilon$. For every real $\mu\ge0$, put $k=\lfloor\mu\rfloor$ and $\rho=\mu-k$, and define

$$J_s(\mu)=(1-\rho)(k)_s+\rho(k+1)_s.$$

For every integer $r\ge1$, let $\widetilde\Gamma_r^P$ and $\widetilde\Gamma_r^L$ be the analogous all-accepted probabilities when the incident lines or points are sampled independently with replacement. Then

$$\Gamma_s^P\ge\frac{J_s((p+1)\epsilon)}{(p+1)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$

$$\Gamma_s^L\ge\frac{J_s(p\epsilon)}{(p)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p),$$

and

$$\widetilde\Gamma_r^P\ge\epsilon^r,\qquad \widetilde\Gamma_r^L\ge\epsilon^r\qquad(r\ge1).$$

researcher-0009:P3.1 [proved; polished] Affine carrier
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Define

$$S_d=\{0,1,\ldots,d-1\}\subset\mathbb F_p,\qquad f(x,y)=0.$$

For every $m,b\in\mathbb F_p$, parameterize the nonvertical line $L_{m,b}$ by $L_{m,b}(t)=(t,mt+b)$ and assign it the label

$$P_{m,b}(t)=\prod_{s\in S_d}(t+m-s).$$

For every $c\in\mathbb F_p$, parameterize the vertical line $V_c$ by $V_c(t)=(c,t)$ and assign it the label

$$P_{V_c}(t)=\prod_{s\in S_d}(t-s).$$

These data form a valid affine line-versus-point table of agreement $\epsilon_0=d/p$. Every label has formal degree exactly $d$, every affine line has accepted degree $d$, exactly $pd$ points have accepted degree $d+1$, and the remaining $p(p-d)$ points have accepted degree $d$.

researcher-0009:P4.1 [proved; polished] Carrier graph
DEPS=P3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let the valid table be the table defined in P3. Its accepted bipartite point-line graph is connected, and every point of $\mathbb F_p^2$ and every affine line is a nonisolated vertex.

researcher-0009:P5.1 [proved; polished] Carrier moments
DEPS=P2,P3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let the table defined in P3 have agreement $\epsilon_0=d/p$. Then

$$\Gamma_s^P=\frac{(1-d/p)(d)_s+(d/p)(d+1)_s}{(p+1)_s}=\frac{J_s((p+1)\epsilon_0)}{(p+1)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p+1),$$

$$\Gamma_s^L=\frac{(d)_s}{(p)_s}=\frac{J_s(p\epsilon_0)}{(p)_s}\qquad(0\le s\le p),$$

and, for every integer $r\ge1$,

$$\widetilde\Gamma_r^L=\epsilon_0^r.$$

researcher-0009:P6.1 [proved; polished] Fractional reaching
DEPS=P3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let the table defined in P3 have accepted edge set $E$ and agreement $\epsilon_0=d/p$. For nonnegative real line weights $\lambda=(\lambda_M)$, define

$$u_{x,L}(\lambda)=\min\left\{1,\sum_{\substack{M\ni x,\ M\ne L\\(x,M)\in E}}\lambda_M\right\}.$$

For $0<\theta\le1$, call $\lambda$ $\theta$-reaching if

$$\sum_{x:(x,L)\in E}u_{x,L}(\lambda)\ge\theta d$$

for every affine line $L$, and let $\operatorname{OPT}_\theta$ be the minimum of $\sum_M\lambda_M$ over all such weights. Then

$$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

researcher-0009:P7.1 [proved; polished] Affine incidence neighborhoods
DEPS=
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $G$ be the bipartite accepted-incidence graph of a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$. Then $G$ contains no $K_{2,2}$, and every set of at least two distinct vertices on either shore of $G$ has at most one common neighbor.

researcher-0009:P8.1 [proved; polished] Compatibility graph degrees
DEPS=P1,P2
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have local agreement $\epsilon$. For each point $x$, let $a_x$ be its accepted-line degree, put $\mu=(p+1)\epsilon$ and $N=p(p+1)$, and let $H$ be the graph on the $N$ affine lines in which two distinct nonparallel lines are adjacent exactly when both accept their unique intersection. If $\bar\Delta=2e(H)/N$, then

$$e(H)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}\binom{a_x}{2}$$

and

$$\bar\Delta\ge\frac{p\mu(\mu-1)}{p+1}.$$

researcher-0009:P9.1 [proved; polished] Auxiliary neighborhood moments
DEPS=P8
Under the hypotheses and notation of P8, let $t$ be an integer with $1\le t\le\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor$, and define

$$M_t(H)=\frac{\sum_{L\in V(H)}\binom{\deg_H(L)}t}{\binom Nt}.$$

There is a set $S\subseteq V(H)$ of $t$ distinct lines such that

$$\left|\bigcap_{M\in S}N_H(M)\right|\ge M_t(H)\ge N\frac{\binom{\lfloor\bar\Delta\rfloor}t}{\binom Nt}.$$

If additionally $\mu\ge2$ and $\bar\Delta\ge2t+2$, then

$$M_t(H)\ge N\left(\frac{\epsilon^2}{4}\right)^t.$$

researcher-0009:P10.1 [proved; polished] Seed-intersection concurrency
DEPS=P9
Under the hypotheses and notation of P9, fix one $t$-line seed set $S$ supplied there, and write

$$N_H(S)=\bigcap_{M\in S}N_H(M).$$

For $L\in N_H(S)$, define

$$h_L=\left|\{L\cap M:M\in S\}\right|.$$

For this fixed $S$, every integer $h$ with $1\le h\le t$ satisfies

$$\left|\{L\in N_H(S):h_L\ge h\}\right|\ge M_t(H)-\frac{\binom t2(p+1)}{t-h+1}.$$

researcher-0009:P11.1 [proved; polished] DRC parameter comparison
DEPS=P10,KTZ Lemmas 2.2 and 4.1--4.3
Fix an integer $d\ge101$, an integer $t\ge1$, and a constant $C>0$. Put

$$D_t=16(\lfloor\sqrt{dt}\rfloor+1).$$

Let $h_L$ be the number of distinct seed intersections on a common-neighbor target line for a $t$-line seed set, as in P10. If transfer requires $h_L>D_t$, then necessarily $t>256d$. For primes $p>d$, put

$$\epsilon_p=C\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3},\qquad N_p=p(p+1).$$

For every fixed $d$, $t>256d$, and $C$,

$$N_p\left(\frac{\epsilon_p^2}{4}\right)^t=O_{C,d,t}\left(p^{2-2t/3}\right)\longrightarrow0$$

as $p\to\infty$ through primes.

researcher-0009:P12.1 [proved; polished] Carrier scope
DEPS=P3,P4,P5,P6,P7
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and set $\epsilon_0=d/p$. There exists a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ whose accepted carrier is connected and has positive degree at every affine point and line, which attains both distinct-star moment lower envelopes for every point-centered order $0\le s\le p+1$ and every line-centered order $0\le s\le p$, and whose line-centered with-replacement moment is $\epsilon_0^r$ for every integer $r\ge1$. For every $0<\theta\le1$, the same table satisfies

$$\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}\le\operatorname{OPT}_\theta\le\frac{\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

For this table, the polynomial $Q=0$ has total degree $0\le d$ and agrees with the point table at every point.

researcher-0009:P13.1 [proved; polished] KTZ parameter chain
DEPS=KTZ Theorem 1.1,KTZ Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1--4.3
Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have point function $f$ and agreement $0<\epsilon\le1$. Suppose the KTZ selector has size $|R|=O(p/\epsilon)$, weighted interpolation uses degree $D=O(\sqrt{d|R|})$, and transfer and lifting require the resulting interpolation scale to fit the budget $D=O(\epsilon p)$. Then the interpolation scale is $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$, and compatibility of these scales requires

$$\epsilon^3=\Omega\left(\frac dp\right).$$

If additionally

$$\epsilon\ge C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3}$$

for the sufficiently large absolute constant in KTZ Theorem 1.1, then there is a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ and an absolute constant $c>0$ such that

$$\frac{|\{x\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\}|}{p^2}\ge c\epsilon.$$

researcher-0010:[P1].1 [proved; polished] Asymmetric Mixing Bounds
DEPS=KTZ Lemma 2.1
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a nonempty accepted point-line subgraph over $\mathbb F_p^2$. Suppose every point of $X$ has degree at least $u$, every line of $T$ has degree at least $v$, $t=|T|$, $\tau=t/[p(p+1)]$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If

$$
uv\ge2^{60}dp
\quad\text{and}\quad
uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,
$$

then

$$
\tau\ge\frac{u}{4(p+1)},\qquad
\frac{|X|}{p^2}\ge\frac{v}{4p},\qquad
w^2\ge2^{57}dp.
$$

researcher-0010:[P2].1 [proved; polished] Line Selector
DEPS=[P1],Chernoff bounds
Under the hypotheses of the asymmetric mixing bounds, there exists $R\subseteq T$ such that

$$
\frac{4t}{u}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{u}
\quad\text{and}\quad
|R|\ge p,
$$

and, for every $L\in T$, more than half of the points $x\in N_G(L)$ are incident to a line $M\in R\setminus\{L\}$ with $(x,M)\in E$.

researcher-0010:[P3].1 [proved; polished] Squarefree Explainer

$$
D=16\bigl(\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\rfloor+1\bigr).

There exists a nonzero squarefree, $Z$-dependent polynomial $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that

$$
A_Z\not\equiv0,
\qquad

$$

for every $L\in R$, and

$$
D<\frac{\min\{u,v\}}{5000}<p.
$$

researcher-0010:[P4].1 [proved; polished] Identity Extension

Let $A$ and $D$ be as in the squarefree explainer entry, and let $R$ satisfy the selector property. Then

A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in T$.

researcher-0010:[P5].1 [proved; polished] Simple-Root Cleanup
DEPS=[P4],KTZ Lemma 2.6
Let $A$ be the squarefree explainer whose identity holds on every line of $T$, and write $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the greatest common divisor of the $Z$-coefficients of $A$. Then $B$ is squarefree, $Z$-primitive, $Z$-dependent, has weighted degree at most $D<p$, and satisfies

$$
\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.
$$

After deleting at most

$$
pD^2+Dt
$$

accepted edges, every retained edge $(x,L)$ satisfies

$$
B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad
B(x,f(x))=0,
\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$

researcher-0010:[P6].1 [proved; polished] Asymmetric Pruning
DEPS=[P1],[P3],[P5]
Under the hypotheses of the asymmetric mixing bounds, remove the exceptional edges from the simple-root cleanup and set

$$
u_1=\left\lfloor\frac{u}{100}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
v_1=\left\lfloor\frac{v}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Iteratively deleting points of current degree below $u_1$ and lines of current degree below $v_1$ leaves a nonempty subgraph in which every retained edge is a simple root of $B$, the point minimum degree is at least $u_1$, and the line minimum degree is at least $v_1$. Moreover,

$$
u_1\ge\frac{u}{200},
\qquad
v_1\ge\frac{v}{200},
\qquad
u_1,v_1>D.
$$

researcher-0010:[P7].1 [proved; polished] Component Recovery
DEPS=[P1],[P6],KTZ Lemmas 6.1 and 6.3
Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a nonempty accepted subgraph with point minimum degree $u$, line minimum degree $v$, line measure $\tau=|T|/[p(p+1)]$, and $w=\min\{u,v\}$. If

$$
uv\ge2^{60}dp
\quad\text{and}\quad
uw^2\ge2^{60}d\tau p^2,
$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{v}{800p}.
$$

researcher-0010:[P8].1 [conditional; polished] Popularity-Tail Translation
DEPS=[P7]
Let the full accepted graph have normalized edge mass $\epsilon>0$, let $a_x$ be the accepted degree of $x$, and define

$$
\delta_j=\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)}\sum_{x:a_x\ge j}a_x,
\qquad
u_*=\max\{j\in\{1,\ldots,p+1\}:\delta_j\ge\epsilon/2\}.
$$

If

$$
u_*\epsilon^2\ge2^{70}d,
$$

then there exists a polynomial $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying

$$
\operatorname{agr}(f,Q)\ge\frac{\epsilon}{12800}.
$$

If additionally $0\le a\le1$, $0<\kappa\le1$, and $u_*\ge\kappa\epsilon^ap$, the same conclusion holds whenever

$$
\epsilon\ge
\left(\frac{2^{70}}{\kappa}\right)^{1/(a+2)}
\left(\frac{d}{p}\right)^{1/(a+2)}.
$$

researcher-0010:[P9].1 [proved; polished] Template Popularity
DEPS=KTZ Lemma 2.1,Cauchy-Schwarz
Let $T_1,\ldots,T_R$ be disjoint affine-line families, and let $Q_1,\ldots,Q_R\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ have total degree at most $d$. Suppose

$$
P_L=Q_j|_L
$$

for every $L\in T_j$. Define

$$
\tau_j=\frac{|T_j|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_{j=1}^R\tau_j,
\qquad
\alpha=\max_{1\le j\le R}\operatorname{agr}(f,Q_j),
$$

and let $\delta>0$ be the normalized accepted-incidence mass carried by $\bigcup_jT_j$. Then

$$
\delta\le\alpha\tau+
\sqrt{\frac{R\alpha\tau}{p+1}}.
$$

If $R\le\delta(p+1)$, then $\alpha\ge\delta/4$.

researcher-0010:[P10].1 [proved; polished] Constant-Label Decoding
DEPS=[P9]
Let $T$ be a family of constant-labelled affine lines, let

$$
\tau=\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},
$$

let $\delta>0$ be its normalized accepted-incidence mass, and put

$$
\mu=\max_{a\in\mathbb F_p}\Pr_x[f(x)=a].
$$

Then

$$
\delta\le\mu\tau+
\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{p+1}}.
$$

If $\delta\ge2\sqrt{\tau/(p+1)}$, then

$$
\mu\ge\frac{\delta}{2\tau}\ge\frac{\delta}{2}.
$$

If these lines carry at least a $\theta$ fraction of total agreement and

$$
\epsilon\ge\frac{2}{\theta\sqrt{p+1}},
$$

then some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\theta\epsilon/2$.

researcher-0010:[P11].1 [proved; polished] Star-Moment Identities
DEPS=
Let $a_x$ be the accepted degree of $x$ in a deterministic table over $\mathbb F_p^2$. For $2\le s\le p+1$, let $\Gamma_s$ be the probability that $s$ ordered distinct uniformly sampled lines through a uniformly sampled point all accept there, and write

$$
(n)_s=n(n-1)\cdots(n-s+1).
$$

Then

$$
\Gamma_s=\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)_s}\sum_x(a_x)_s.
$$

If

$$
\mu=\epsilon(p+1)=k+\theta,
\qquad
k=\lfloor\mu\rfloor,
\qquad
0\le\theta<1,
$$

then

$$
\Gamma_s\ge
\frac{(1-\theta)(k)_s+\theta(k+1)_s}{(p+1)_s}.
$$

For $s=2$,

$$
\Gamma_2=\epsilon^2-
\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{p}+
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.
$$

researcher-0010:[P12].1 [proved; polished] Modal Occupancy Concentration
DEPS=Second-moment inequality,McDiarmid inequality
Let $p$ be prime and set

$$
r=\left\lfloor\frac{\log p}{4\log\log p}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
u_0=\left\lceil\frac{4\log p}{\log\log p}\right\rceil.
$$

Assume

$$
r\ge133,
\qquad
r^2\le p,
\qquad
p\ge8r!,
\qquad
u_0+1\le17r,
\qquad
\frac{2p}{(u_0+1)!}\le1.
$$

Choose the values $f(x)$ independently and uniformly from $\mathbb F_p$. On each affine line, choose a modal value uniformly using an independent tie-breaking coin and use it as the constant line polynomial. If $M$ is the maximum occupancy of $p$ independent uniform samples from $\mathbb F_p$, $\epsilon$ is the realized local agreement, and

$$
q=\frac{\mathbb E[M]}p=\mathbb E[\epsilon],
$$

then

$$
\frac{r}{65p}\le q\le\frac{17r}{p}
$$

and

$$
\Pr\left[\frac q2\le\epsilon\le\frac{3q}{2}\right]\ge\frac34.
$$

researcher-0010:[P13].1 [proved; polished] Modal Table Realization
DEPS=[P12],Chernoff bound,Markov inequality
Fix $S\ge2$. Let $p$ be prime and set

$$
r=\left\lfloor\frac{\log p}{4\log\log p}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
u_0=\left\lceil\frac{4\log p}{\log\log p}\right\rceil,
\qquad
v_0=\left\lceil\frac{8\log p}{\log\log p}\right\rceil.
$$

Assume

$$
r\ge133,
\quad r^2\le p,
\quad p\ge8r!,
\quad u_0+1\le17r,
\quad v_0\le33r,
$$

$$
\frac{2p}{(u_0+1)!}\le1,
\quad
\frac{2p^3}{v_0!}\le\frac18,
\quad
p^{5253}(e/4)^p\le\frac18,
\quad
S\le p+1.
$$

At ambient degree $d=101$, there exists a deterministic table with constant line polynomials such that

$$
\frac{r}{130p}\le\epsilon\le\frac{26r}{p},
$$

$$
\max_{\substack{Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]\\ \deg Q\le101}}
\operatorname{agr}(f,Q)<\frac2p,
$$

and, for every $2\le s\le S$,

$$
\Gamma_s\le2^{s+2}(S-1)\epsilon^s.
$$

Every affine line has at least one and fewer than $v_0$ accepted points.

researcher-0010:[P14].1 [refuted; polished] Scale-Free Star-Energy Implication
DEPS=[P13]
Fix an integer $s\ge2$, a real number $\gamma>0$, and constants $c_0,c_1>0$. Let a deterministic line-versus-point table over $\mathbb F_p^2$ have ambient degree at most $101$ and local agreement $\epsilon>0$. If

$$
\max_{\substack{Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]\\ \deg Q\le101}}
\operatorname{agr}(f,Q)
\le c_0\epsilon,
$$

then

$$
\Gamma_s\ge c_1\epsilon^{s-\gamma}.
$$

ROUND 2
SUMMARY Round 2 preserves the exact accepted KTZ component chain `E2E-04`–`E2E-12`, whose available unconditional machinery stops at the square-root frontier. The near-linear spine is `E2E-15`–`E2E-24`: an algebra-aware rank-or-decode dichotomy must retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass while producing weighted degree $D=O_\theta(d)$. That bound makes the accepted whole-relation cleanup loss $O_\theta(d/p)$ absorbable and leaves only $O_\theta(1)$ irreducible factors. A heavy simple factor then linearizes as $Z-Q$, and all of its edges—across disconnected components—charge to one global polynomial without Vinh mixing. The factor lift is mathematically supported but lacks a fresh exact-accepted standalone source. Accepted round-2 singular-pencil examples show that its simple-root hypothesis cannot be weakened to absolute irreducibility, smooth resultants, or high direction count alone. The goal remains blocked on the low-weight incidence dichotomy and exact audits of the strengthened algebraic transfers; the uniform $Cd/p$ endpoint remains separately refuted.

ROUND 3
SUMMARY Round 3 retains the independently accepted KTZ component chain `E2E-04`–`E2E-12`, whose available unconditional machinery still stops at a conditional square-root frontier. The near-linear spine is `E2E-15`–`E2E-24`: a label-aware rank-or-decode dichotomy must retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass while producing weighted degree $D=O_\theta(d)$. The rank-reaching definition now correctly allows $D\ge d$, with $D<p$ derived downstream. The main structural simplification is `E2E-21`–`E2E-23`: lift the common cleaned relation separately on every connected component, observe that each resulting $Q_\Gamma$ contributes the divisor $Z-Q_\Gamma$ of the same polynomial $B$, and hence bound the number of distinct component polynomials by $\deg_ZB\le D/d$. Elementary edge pigeonholing then yields one global polynomial across all components, avoiding both Vinh mixing and the former standalone irreducible seed-factor lift. The target remains blocked on `E2E-15` and standalone audits of the generalized algebraic transfers; the uniform $Cd/p$ endpoint remains separately refuted.
